Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I’ve been snapping at people. At work, during a safety meeting, I started yelling before I could stop myself. Now some of the younger inspectors seem scared to even ask me anything.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those outbursts? Do you feel like you can control them when they happen, or does it feel like you just can't help yourself in the moment?\n\nPatient: It feels like I can’t really catch it in time. I’ll be fine enough, then someone says something and I’m already yelling. Like that safety meeting… I knew I was doing it, but I couldn’t seem to stop myself. Now people are careful around me, especially the younger inspectors.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself acting on other impulses that you later regret, like sudden decisions or actions you can’t explain?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really. I mean, I say things before I think, but big sudden decisions or anything like that… I haven’t noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, irritable, or having trouble managing your mood lately, or is this mostly about the outbursts during specific situations?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly the snapping, I think. I get irritable fast in certain situations… like that safety meeting, I just started yelling before I could stop myself. Now some of the younger inspectors seem afraid to come to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling irritable or on edge most of the time, even when you’re not in situations that trigger these outbursts?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more than I want to admit. I’m kind of on edge a lot, and then it doesn’t take much before I snap. That’s why I’m here. Like at that safety meeting, I started yelling before I could stop myself, and now the younger inspectors seem scared to ask me anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt unusually energetic, more confident, or needed less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I haven’t had times where I felt extra energetic or like I didn’t need sleep. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt down or hopeless for several days or more, even before these outbursts started happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really something I’ve kept track of. I mean, I’ve had bad days, sure, but I’m not sure about several days of feeling hopeless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you had to act on these impulses, like you couldn’t resist the urge to yell or get angry, even if you knew it wasn’t the right reaction?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it feels like that sometimes. Like I know I shouldn’t raise my voice, but it’s already coming out before I can stop it. That safety meeting was like that. I just snapped, and now some of the younger inspectors barely want to ask me anything.\n\nDoctor: Has your anger or these outbursts ever caused problems with your relationships or your work before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I’ve missed a lot of shifts, and at work I guess I’ve gotten short with the trainees enough that they don’t really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore.\n\nAnd… outside of work, I stopped doing community theater. I can’t count on myself to get through rehearsals without something happening, so I just pulled back.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your reactions are out of proportion to the situation, especially with anger, or do they usually feel justified to you at the time?\n\nPatient: At the time it feels justified, I guess… but afterward, yeah, I can see it’s too much. Like that safety meeting—I started yelling before I could stop myself. Now the younger inspectors seem scared to even ask me things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting quickly without thinking through the consequences, even outside of these anger episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a way I’ve noticed. It’s more when I’m already worked up. Outside of that, I try to think things through.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been aggressive toward others or animals, like starting physical fights or hurting someone or an animal on purpose?\n\nPatient: Not physical fights, no… I haven’t hurt an animal or anything like that. I mean, I’ve had bad days, sure, but I’m not sure about several days of feeling hopeless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you had to act on these impulses, like you couldn’t resist the urge to yell or get angry, even if you knew it wasn’t the right reaction?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it feels like that sometimes. I just snapped, and now some of the younger inspectors barely want to ask me anything.\n\nDoctor: Has your anger or these outbursts ever caused problems with your relationships or your work before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I’ve missed a lot of shifts, and at work I guess I’ve gotten short with the trainees enough that they don’t really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore.\n\nAnd… outside of work, I stopped doing community theater. I can’t count on myself to get through rehearsals without something happening, so I just pulled back.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your reactions are out of proportion to the situation, especially with anger, or do they usually feel justified to you at the time?\n\nPatient: At the time it feels justified, I guess… but afterward, yeah, I can see it’s too much. Like that safety meeting—I started yelling before I could stop myself. Now the younger inspectors seem scared to even ask me things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself acting quickly without thinking through the consequences, even outside of these anger episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a way I’ve noticed. It’s more when I’m already worked up. Outside of that, I try to think things through.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been aggressive toward others or animals, like starting physical fights or hurting someone or an animal on purpose?\n\nPatient: Not physical fights, no… I haven’t hurt an animal or anything like that. It’s more I snap at people—yelling, getting too sharp. Like that safety meeting… I started yelling before I could stop myself, and now the younger inspectors are scared to ask me things.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, did you have any significant behavior problems or get in trouble for things like aggression, rule-breaking, or defiance when you were a child, before age 10?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t remember anything like that being a big issue… not before I was 10.\n\nDoctor: Have you had multiple episodes of being unable to control your anger, like sudden outbursts that seem extreme compared to the situation, even outside of work settings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. It’s not just work. I keep snapping at people, and sometimes it comes out bigger than it should. Like that safety meeting—I started yelling before I could stop myself. Now the younger inspectors seem scared to even ask me things.\n\nDoctor: Has your mood been consistently irritable or angry even when you’re not having these outbursts?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I’m not yelling all the time, but I’m on edge a lot. People ask me something simple and I just… snap. That’s part of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do these anger outbursts and feeling irritable happen almost every day, or are there days where you feel more in control and less on edge?\n\nPatient: It’s… most days, I think. Now some of the younger inspectors seem scared to even ask me anything.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work issues and avoiding community theater, have these outbursts affected your personal relationships or daily life in other important ways, like with your family or close friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly it’s been work, and I’ve backed out of theater because I can’t count on myself to get through rehearsals. I’ve pulled back some.\n\nDoctor: When you feel yourself starting to get angry, do you feel like you completely lose control of your actions, or is there still some part of you that feels like you could stop if you tried harder?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… in the moment it feels like I’m already doing it before I’ve decided anything.
